v\in V(G)

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})(v)-\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x}^{\prime})(v)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\|\mathcal{x}|_{N[v]}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}|_{N[v]}\right\|_{\infty}\max\{\deg(v),1\}

(28)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\|\mathcal{x}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}|G|.

(29)

Proof 4.5
.

Again, the local bound (
28
) implies the global bound
(
29
). So we only need to prove (
28
).
Let

\textsf{msg},\textsf{agg},\textsf{comb}
be the message, aggregation, and combination
functions of
{\mathfrak{L}}
. Let
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{msg}}
and
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{comb}}
be FNNs
computing
msg
and
comb
, respectively, and let

\lambda_{\textsf{msg}}\coloneqq\lambda({\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{msg}})
and

\lambda_{\textsf{comb}}\coloneq\lambda({\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{comb}})
be their Lipschitz constants
(from Lemma
2.6
(1)).

Let
G
be a graph,
\mathcal{x},\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
, and

\mathcal{y}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})
,

\mathcal{y}^{\prime}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x}^{\prime})
. Let
v\in V(G)
.
For all
w\in N(v)
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\textsf{msg}(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{x}(w))-\textsf{msg}(\mathcal{x}^{\prime}(v),\mathcal{x}^{\prime}(w))\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\textsf{msg}}\left\|(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{x}(w))-(\mathcal{x}^{\prime}(v),\mathcal{x}^{\prime}(w))\right\|_{\infty}.

Thus for

\displaystyle\mathcal{z}(v)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\textsf{agg}\Big({\big\{\hskip-4.3pt\big\{}\textsf{msg}(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{x}(w))\mathrel{\big|}w\in N_{G}(v){\big\}\hskip-4.3pt\big\}}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{z}^{\prime}(v)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\textsf{agg}\Big({\big\{\hskip-4.3pt\big\{}\textsf{msg}(\mathcal{x}^{\prime}(v),\mathcal{x}^{\prime}(w))\mathrel{\big|}w\in N_{G}(v){\big\}\hskip-4.3pt\big\}}\Big)

we have

\left\|\mathcal{z}(v)-\mathcal{z}^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{\textsf{msg}}\left\|\mathcal{x}|_{N[v]}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}|_{N[v]}\right\|_{\infty}\deg(v).

(30)

It follows that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{y}(v)-\mathcal{y}^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\textsf{comb}(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}(v))-\textsf{comb}(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\textsf{comb}}\left\|(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}(v))-(\mathcal{x}^{\prime}(v),\mathcal{z}^{\prime}(v))\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\textsf{comb}}\max\Big\{\left\|\mathcal{x}(v)-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\mathcal{z}(v)-\mathcal{z}^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\textsf{comb}}\lambda_{\textsf{msg}}\left\|\mathcal{x}|_{N[v]}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}|_{N[v]}\right\|_{\infty}\max\{\deg(v),1\}.

(31)

This implies the assertion of the lemma for

\lambda\coloneqq\lambda_{\textsf{comb}}\lambda_{\textsf{msg}}
.

5
The Uniform Case: GNNs with Rational Weights

In this section, we study the descriptive complexity of rational
piecewise linear GNNs. The following theorem, which is the main result
of this section, states that the signal transformations computed by
rational piecewise linear GNNs can be approximated arbitrarily closely by
GFO+C
-formulas and
terms.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
{\mathfrak{N}}
be a rational piecewise linear GNN of input dimension
p

and output dimension
q
. Let
\boldsymbol{X}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{X}_{p}
be
r-schemas of type
{\texttt{v}\to\texttt{r}}
, and let
W
be a function variable
of type
\texttt{v}\to\texttt{n}
.
Then there are guarded
r-expressions
\textsf{gnn-eval}_{1}(x),\ldots,\textsf{gnn-eval}_{q}(x)
such that the following holds for all graphs
G
and assignments

\mathcal{a}
over
G
. Let
\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
be the signal defined by

\mathcal{x}(v)\coloneqq\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{p}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big),

(32)

and let
\mathcal{y}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}(G,\mathcal{x})
. Then for
all
v\in V(G)
,

\left\|\mathcal{y}(v)-\big(\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}_{q}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\big)\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{-\mathcal{a}(W)(v)}.

(33)

The main step in the proof of the theorem is the following lemma,
which is the analogue of the theorem for a single GNN layer.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
{\mathfrak{L}}
be a rational piecewise linear GNN layer of input dimension
p

and output dimension
q
. Let
\boldsymbol{X}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{X}_{p}
be
r-schemas of type
{\texttt{v}\to\texttt{r}}
, and let
W
be a function variable
of type
\texttt{v}\to\texttt{n}
. Then there are guarded
r-expressions
\textsf{l-eval}_{1}(x),\ldots,\textsf{l-eval}_{q}(x)
such
that the following holds for all graphs
G
and assignments

\mathcal{a}
over
G
.
Let
\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
be the signal defined by

\mathcal{x}(v)\coloneqq\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{p}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big)

(34)

and let
\mathcal{y}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})
. Then for
all
v\in V(G)
,

\left\|\mathcal{y}(v)-\big(\left\llangle\textsf{l-eval}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\textsf{l-eval}_{q}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\big)\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{-\mathcal{a}(W)(v)}

(35)

Proof 5.3
.

For the presentation of the proof it will be easiest to fix a graph

G
and an assignment
\mathcal{a}
over
G
, though of course the formulas
and terms we shall define will not depend on this graph and
assignment. Let
\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
be the signal defined in
(
34
), and let
\mathcal{y}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})
. Let

\textsf{msg}:{\mathbb{R}}^{2p}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r}
,
agg
, and

\textsf{comb}:{\mathbb{R}}^{p+r}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{q}
be the message, aggregation, and
combination functions of
{\mathfrak{L}}
. Let
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{msg}}
and
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{comb}}

be rational piecewise linear FNNs computing
\textsf{msg},\textsf{comb}
,
respectively, and recall that
agg
is either
SUM
,
MEAN
, or

MAX
defined on finite multisets of vectors in
{\mathbb{R}}^{r}
. Let

\lambda\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
be a Lipschitz constant for
comb
.

{techclaim}

There are guarded r-expressions

\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1}(x,x^{\prime}),\ldots,\boldsymbol{\mu}_{r}(x,x^{\prime})
such that for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V(G)

\displaystyle\textsf{msg}\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{p}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v^{\prime}),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{p}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v^{\prime})\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt=\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,v^{\prime}),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{r}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,v^{\prime})\Big)

{subproof}

This follows from Corollary
3.42
applied to

{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{msg}}
. The expressions we obtain are guarded, because we
just substitute atoms containing the variables
x
or
x^{\prime}
in the
arithmetical formulas we obtain from Corollary
3.42
, but
never quantify over vertex variables.

{techclaim}

Let
\boldsymbol{Z}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{Z}_{r}
be r-schemas of type

{\texttt{v}\to\texttt{r}}
. Then there are guarded r-expressions

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1}(x),\ldots,\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{q}(x)
such that for all assignments
\mathcal{a}^{\prime}

over
G
,

\displaystyle\textsf{comb}\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{p}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\left\llangle\boldsymbol{Z}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{Z}_{r}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt=\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{q}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big)

{subproof}

Again, this follows from Corollary
3.42
.

To complete the proof, we need to distinguish between the different
aggregation functions.
MEAN
-aggregation is most problematic,
because it involves a division, which we can only approximate in our
logic.

Case 1:

\textsf{agg}=\textsf{SUM}
.

We substitute the r-expressions

\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}(x,x^{\prime})
of Claim
5.3
for the r-schema
\boldsymbol{Z}
in the
r-expression
itadd
of Lemma
3.53
to obtain guarded r-expressions

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}
, for
i\in[r]
, such that for all
v\in V(G)

we have

\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)=\sum_{v^{\prime}\in N(v)}\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,v^{\prime}).

(36)

Then we substitute the r-expressions
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(x)
for
the variables
\boldsymbol{Z}_{i}
in the formulas
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}
of
Claim
5.3
and obtain the desired r-expressions
\textsf{l-eval}_{j}(x)

such that

\displaystyle\Big(\left\llangle\textsf{l-eval}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\textsf{l-eval}_{q}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\textsf{comb}\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{p}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{r}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\mathcal{y}(v).

Thus in this case, the r-expressions
\textsf{l-eval}_{j}(x)
even
define
\mathcal{y}
exactly. Of course this implies that they satisfy
(
35
).

Case 2:

\textsf{agg}=\textsf{MAX}
.

We can argue as in Case 1, using the r-expression
max
of
Lemma
3.53
instead of
itadd
. Again, we obtain
r-expressions
\textsf{l-eval}_{j}(x)
that define
\mathcal{y}
exactly.

Case 3:

\textsf{agg}=\textsf{MEAN}
.

The proof is similar to Case 1 and Case 2, but we need
to be careful. We cannot define the mean of a family of
numbers exactly, but only approximately, because of the division it
involves.

Exactly as in Case 1 we define r-expressions

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(x)
satisfying (
36
). Recall that

\lambda
is a Lipschitz constant for
comb
. Let
\boldsymbol{\delta}(x)\coloneqq\#x^{\prime}.E(x,x^{\prime})
be a term defining the degree of a vertex. Using
Lemma
3.35
we can construct an r-expression

\boldsymbol{\nu}_{i}
such that

\left|\frac{\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)}{\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\delta}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)}-\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{i}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\right|<2^{-\mathcal{a}(W)-\lambda}\leq\lambda^{-1}2^{-\mathcal{a}(W)}

if
\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\delta}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)=\deg(v)\neq 0
and
\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{i}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)=0
otherwise. Thus, letting

\displaystyle\mathcal{z}(v)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\textsf{MEAN}\big({\big\{\hskip-4.3pt\big\{}\textsf{msg}(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{x}(v^{\prime}))\mathrel{\big|}v^{\prime}\in N(v){\big\}\hskip-4.3pt\big\}}\big)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\deg(v)=0,\\
\left(\frac{\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)}{\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\delta}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)},\ldots,\frac{\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{r}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)}{\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\delta}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)}\right)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

we have

\left\|\mathcal{z}(v)-\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{r}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda^{-1}2^{-\mathcal{a}(W)}

for all
v\in V(G)
. By the Lipschitz continuity of
comb
, this
implies

\left\|\textsf{comb}\big(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}(v)\big)-\textsf{comb}\Big(\mathcal{x}(v),\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{r}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big)\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{-\mathcal{a}(W)}.

(37)

We substitute the r-expressions
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{i}(x)
for
the variables
\boldsymbol{Z}_{i}
in the formulas
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}
of
Claim
5.3
and obtain r-expressions
\textsf{l-eval}_{j}(x)

such that

\displaystyle\Big(\left\llangle\textsf{l-eval}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\textsf{l-eval}_{q}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\textsf{comb}\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{p}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{r}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\textsf{comb}\Big(\mathcal{x}(v),\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{r}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big).

Since
\mathcal{y}(v)=\textsf{comb}\big(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}(v)\big)
, the assertion
(
35
) follows from (
37
).

Proof 5.4

(Proof of Theorem
5.1
)
.

We fix a graph
G
and assignment
\mathcal{a}
over
G
for the
presentation of the proof; as usual the formulas we shall define will not
depend on this graph and assignment. Let
\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
be the
signal defined in (
32
).

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{N}}=({\mathfrak{L}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\mathfrak{L}}^{(d)})
. Let
p^{(i-1)}
be
the input dimension of
{\mathfrak{L}}^{(i)}
, and let
p^{(i)}
be the output
dimension. Then
p=p^{(0)}
and
q=p^{(d)}
.
Moreover, let

\mathcal{x}^{(0)}\coloneqq\mathcal{x}
and

\mathcal{x}^{(i)}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{(i)}(G,\mathcal{x}^{(i-1)})
for

i\in[d]
. Note that
\mathcal{x}^{(d)}=\mathcal{y}
.

For every
i\in[d]
, let
\lambda^{(i)}\coloneqq\lambda({\mathfrak{L}}^{(i)})

be the constant of Lemma
4.4
. We inductively define
a sequence of
GFO+C
-terms
\textsf{err}^{(i)}(x)
, which will give us the
desired error bounds. We let
\textsf{err}^{(d)}(x)\coloneqq W(x)
. To
define
\textsf{err}^{(i)}(x)
for
0\leq i<d
, we first note that by
Lemma
3.51
, for every
GFO+C
-term
\theta(x)
there is a
GFO+C
-term
\textsf{maxN}_{\theta}(x)
such that for every
v\in V(G)
we have

\left\lsem\textsf{maxN}_{\theta}\right\rsem^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)=\max\Big\{\left\lsem\theta\right\rsem^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(w)\mathrel{\Big|}w\in N_{G}[v]\Big\}.

We let
\textsf{dg}(x)\coloneqq(\#x^{\prime}.E(x,x^{\prime}))+\textsf{1}
and

\textsf{err}^{(i)}(x)\coloneqq\textsf{maxN}_{\textsf{err}^{(i+1)}}(x)+\lambda^{(i+1)}\cdot\textsf{maxN}_{\textsf{dg}}(x)+\textsf{1}.

Letting

k^{(i)}(v)\coloneqq\left\lsem\textsf{err}^{(i)}\right\rsem^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),

for every

v\in V(G)
and
0\leq i<d
, we have

k^{(i)}(v)=\max\big\{k^{(i+1)}(w)\mathrel{\big|}w\in N_{G}[v]\big\}+\lambda^{(i+1)}\max\big\{\deg_{G}(w)+1\mathrel{\big|}w\in N_{G}[v]\big\}+1.

(38)

Furthermore,
k^{(d)}(v)=\mathcal{a}(W)(v)
.

Now for
i\in[d]
and
j\in[p^{(i)}]
we shall define guarded
r-expressions
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{j}^{(i)}(x)
such that for all
v\in V(G)
, with

\mathcal{z}^{(i)}(v)\coloneqq\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}^{(i)}\right\rrangle(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{\rho}_{p^{(i)}}^{(i)}\right\rrangle(v)\Big)

we have

\left\|\mathcal{x}^{(i)}(v)-\mathcal{z}^{(i)}(v)\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{-k^{(i)}(v)}.

(39)

For
i=d
and with
\textsf{gnn-eval}_{j}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(d)}_{j}
, this
implies (
33
) and hence the assertion of the theorem.

To define
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{j}^{(1)}(x)
, we apply
Lemma
5.2
to the first layer
{\mathfrak{L}}^{(1)}
and
substitute
\textsf{err}^{(0)}
for
W
in the
resulting-expression. Then (
39
) for
i=1

follows directly from Lemma
5.2
and the fact that

k^{(0)}(v)\geq k^{(1)}(v)
for all
v
.

For the inductive step, let
2\leq i\leq d
and suppose that we have
defined
\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(i-1)}_{j}(x)
for all
j\in[p^{(i-1)}]
. To define

\boldsymbol{\rho}_{j}^{(i)}(x)
, we apply Lemma
5.2
to the
i
th
layer
{\mathfrak{L}}^{(i)}
and substitute

\boldsymbol{\rho}_{1}^{(i-1)},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\rho}_{p^{(i-1)}}^{(i-1)}
for

\boldsymbol{X}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{X}_{p^{(i-1)}}
and
\textsf{err}^{(i-1)}
for
W
in
the resulting r-expression. Then by Lemma
5.2
, for
all
v\in V(G)
we have

\left\|\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}^{(i)}(G,\mathcal{z}^{(i-1)})(v)-\mathcal{z}^{(i)}(v)\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{-k^{(i-1)}(v)}.

(40)

Moreover, by Lemma
4.4
applied to

{\mathfrak{L}}^{(i)}
and

\mathcal{x}\coloneqq\mathcal{x}^{(i-1)}
,
\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{z}^{(i-1)}
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{x}^{(i)}(v)-\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}^{(i)}(G,\mathcal{z}^{(i-1)})(v)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\lambda^{(i)}\max\left\{\left.\left\|\mathcal{x}^{(i-1)}(w)-\mathcal{z}^{(i-1)}(w)\right\|_{\infty}\;\right|\;w\in N_{G}[v]\right\}\big(\deg_{G}(v)+1\big)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\lambda^{(i)}\max\Big\{2^{-k^{(i-1)}(w)}\mathrel{\Big|}w\in N_{G}[v]\Big\}\big(\deg_{G}(v)+1\big)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\max\Big\{2^{-k^{(i-1)}(w)+\lambda^{(i)}\cdot(\deg_{G}(v)+1)}\mathrel{\Big|}w\in N_{G}[v]\Big\}.

We choose a
w\in N_{G}[v]
minimising
k^{(i-1)}(w)
. Then

\Big\|\mathcal{x}^{(i)}(v)-\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}^{(i)}(G,\mathcal{z}^{(i-1)})(v)\Big\|\leq 2^{-(k^{(i-1)}(w)-\lambda^{(i)}\cdot(\deg_{G}(v)+1))}.

(41)

Combining (
40
) and (
41
) by the triangle
inequality, we get

\big\|\mathcal{x}^{(i)}(v)-\mathcal{z}^{(i)}(v)\big\|\leq 2^{-k^{(i-1)}(v)}+2^{-(k^{(i-1)}(w)-\lambda^{(i)}\cdot(\deg_{G}(v)+1))}.

Observe that
k^{(i-1)}(v)\geq k^{(i)}(v)+1
and

\displaystyle k^{(i-1)}(w)-\lambda^{(i)}\cdot(\deg_{G}(v)+1)

\displaystyle=\max\big\{k^{(i)}(v^{\prime})\mathrel{\big|}v^{\prime}\in N_{G}[w]\big\}+\lambda^{(i)}\max\big\{\deg_{G}(v^{\prime})+1\mathrel{\big|}v^{\prime}\in N_{G}[w]\big\}+1

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\lambda^{(i)}\cdot(\deg_{G}(v)+1)

\displaystyle\geq k^{(i)}(v)+\lambda^{(i)}\big(\deg_{G}(v)+1\big)+1-\lambda^{(i)}\cdot(\deg_{G}(v)+1)\hskip 56.9055pt\text{since
}v\in N_{G}[w]

\displaystyle=k^{(i)}(v)+1.

Thus

\big\|\mathcal{y}^{(i)}(v)-\mathcal{z}^{(i)}(v)\big\|\leq 2^{-k^{(i)}(v)-1}+2^{-k^{(i)}(v)-1}=2^{-k^{(i)}(v)},

which proves (
39
) and hence the theorem.

Remark 5.5
.

It is worth mentioning that the approximation error in
Theorem
5.1
is due to the division involved in MEAN
aggregations. If we only consider rational piecewise linear GNNs
with SUM and MAX aggregation, we obtain an exact simulation by

GFO+C
-formulas, that is, we can
replace inequality (
33
) by the equality

\mathcal{y}(v)=\big(\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}_{q}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\big).

This follows easily from the proof.

However, for MEAN aggregation, we cannot achieve such an exact
result, at least not if we work with dyadic rationals. If we try to
use arbitrary rationals, we run into other difficulties, because the
denominators of the fractions we obtain can get very large.

Since logics define queries, when comparing the expressiveness of graph neural networks with that of
logics, it is best to focus on queries. Recall from
Section
2.5
that we identified

\ell
-labelled graphs with graphs carrying an
\ell
-dimensional
Boolean signal. A unary query on the class
{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}^{\textup{bool}}_{\ell}
is an equivariant signal transformations from

{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}_{\ell}^{\textup{bool}}
to
{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\textup{bool}}
.

We say that a GNN
{\mathfrak{N}}

computes
a unary query
{{\mathcal{Q}}}:{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}_{\ell}^{\textup{bool}}\to{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\textup{bool}}
if for all
(G,\mathcal{b})\in{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}_{\ell}^{\textup{bool}}
and all
v\in V(G)
it holds that

\begin{cases}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}(G,\mathcal{b})(v)\geq\frac{3}{4}&\text{if }{{\mathcal{Q}}}(G,\mathcal{b})(v)=1,\\
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}(G,\mathcal{b})(v)\leq\frac{1}{4}&\text{if }{{\mathcal{Q}}}(G,\mathcal{b})(v)=0.\end{cases}

(42)

Observe that if we allow
\lsig
or
\relu
activations, we can
replace the
\geq\frac{3}{4}
and
\leq\frac{1}{4}
in (
42
) by

=1
and
=0
and thus require

\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}(G,\mathcal{b})(v)={{\mathcal{Q}}}(G,\mathcal{b})(v)
. We simply apply the
transformation
\lsig(2x-\frac{1}{2})
to the output. It maps the interval

(-\infty,\frac{1}{4}]
to
0
and the interval
[\frac{3}{4},\infty)

to
1
. With other activations such as the logistic function, this is
not possible, which is why we chose our more flexible definition.

Corollary 5.6
.

Every unary query on
{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}_{\ell}
that is computable by a rational
piecewise linear GNN is
definable in
GFO+C
.

Note that this Corollary is Theorem
1.4
stated
in the introduction.

The reader may wonder if the converse of the previous corollary holds,
that is, if every query definable in
GFO+C
is computable by a rational
piecewise linear GNN. It is not; we refer the reader to
Remark
7.11
.

As mentioned earlier, there are versions of the theorem for all the
extensions of basic GNNs that we discussed in
Section
4
. For later reference, we state the version for GNNs with global
readout.

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
{\mathfrak{N}}
be a rational piecewise linear GNN with global readout of input dimension
p

and output dimension
q
. Let
\boldsymbol{X}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{X}_{p}
be
r-schemas of type
{\texttt{v}\to\texttt{r}}
, and let
W
be a function variable
of type
\texttt{v}\to\texttt{n}
.
Then there are
r-expressions
\textsf{gnn-eval}_{1}(x)
,
\ldots
,
\textsf{gnn-eval}_{q}(x)

in
\textsf{GFO+C}^{\textup{gc}}
such that the following holds for all graphs
G
and assignments

\mathcal{a}
over
G
. Let
\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
be the signal defined by

\mathcal{x}(v)\coloneqq\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{p}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big),

(43)

and let
\mathcal{y}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}(G,\mathcal{x})
. Then for
all
v\in V(G)
,

\left\|\mathcal{y}(v)-\big(\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}_{q}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\big)\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{-\mathcal{a}(W)(v)}.

(44)

The proof of the this theorem is completely analogous to the proof of
Theorem
5.1
. However, when expressing the global
aggregations (as in (
36
)), we need to use unguarded counting
terms of the shape (
16
) and hence end up with
\textsf{GFO+C}^{\textup{gc}}
-expressions.

Remark 5.8
.

Theorem
5.1
and Corollary
5.6
also have
versions for 1-GNNs and the modal fragment
MFO+C
. In
Theorem
5.1
, if
{\mathfrak{N}}
is a 1-GNN, we can obtain

modal
r-expressions
\textsf{gnn-eval}_{i}(x)
. To prove this,
we need a corresponding version of Lemma
5.2
. In the
proof of this lemma, in Claim
5.3
we have to only need to simulate a
message function

\textsf{msg}\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v^{\prime}),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{p}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v^{\prime})\Big),

dropping the arguments depending on

v
, and hence we only need to construct r-expressions

\mu_{i}(x^{\prime})
. Then in the aggregations, we can apply the modal
version of Lemma
3.53
to obtain modal r-expressions

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}
satisfying (
36
) (in Case 1, and the
corresponding equalities in Cases 2 and 3).

The rest of the proofs of Lemma
5.2
and
Theorem
5.1
go through without any changes. The modal
version of Corollary
5.6
then follows; it reads as:
Every unary query on
{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}_{\ell}
that is computable by a
rational piecewise linear 1-GNN is definable in
MFO+C
.

6
The Non-Uniform Case:
GNNs with Arbitrary Weights and Families of GNNs

Now we consider the general case where the weights in the neural
networks are arbitrary real numbers. We also drop the assumption that
the activation functions be piecewise linear, only requiring rpl
approximability. The price we pay is a non-uniformity on the side of
the logic and a slightly weaker approximation
guarantee as well as a boundedness assumption on the input signal.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
{\mathfrak{N}}
be an rpl-approximable GNN of input dimension
p

and output dimension
q
. Let
\boldsymbol{X}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{X}_{p}
be
r-schemas of type
{\texttt{v}\to\texttt{r}}
, and let
W,W^{\prime}
be function variables
of type
\emptyset\to\texttt{n}
.

Then there are
r-expressions
\textsf{gnn-eval}_{1}(x)
,
\ldots
,
\textsf{gnn-eval}_{q}(x)

in
\textsf{GFO+C}_{\textup{nu}}
such
that the following holds for all graphs
G
and assignments

\mathcal{a}
over
G
.
Let
\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
be the signal defined by

\mathcal{x}(v)\coloneqq\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}_{p}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\Big),

(45)

and let
\mathcal{y}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}(G,\mathcal{x})
. Assume that

\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\mathcal{a}(W)
and that
\mathcal{a}(W^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then for
all
v\in V(G)
,

\left\|\mathcal{y}(v)-\big(\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}_{1}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v),\ldots,\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}_{q}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v)\big)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{\mathcal{a}(W^{\prime})}.

(46)

Let us comment on the role of the two 0-ary functions (that is,
constants)
W,W^{\prime}
. We introduce them to add flexibility in the
bounds. Their values depend on the assignment
\mathcal{a}
, which means that
we can freely choose them. For example, we can let

\mathcal{a}(W)=\mathcal{a}(W^{\prime})=n\coloneqq|G|
. Then we get an approximation error of

1/n
for input signals bounded by
n
. Or we could let
\mathcal{a}(W)=1
and

\mathcal{a}(W^{\prime})=100
. Then we get an approximation error of 1% for Boolean
input signals.

Since we move to a non-uniform regime anyway, to obtain the most
general results we may as well go all
the way to a non-uniform GNN model where we have different GNNs for
every size of the input graphs.

We need additional terminology. We define the
bitsize

\bsize({\mathfrak{F}})
of a rational piecewise linear FNN
{\mathfrak{F}}
to be the sum
of the bitsizes of its skeleton, all its weights and biases, and all
its activations. We define the
weight
of an arbitrary FNN

{\mathfrak{F}}=(V,E,({\mathfrak{a}}_{v})_{v\in V},\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{b})
to be

\wt({\mathfrak{F}})\coloneqq|V|+[E|+\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+\max_{v\in V}\big(\lambda({\mathfrak{a}}_{v})+{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}(0)\big)

Here
\lambda({\mathfrak{a}}_{v})
denotes the least integer that is a Lipschitz
constant for
{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}
.
The
size

\size({\mathfrak{F}})
of a rational piecewise linear FNN
{\mathfrak{F}}

is the maximum of its bitsize and its weight.
The
depth

\depth({\mathfrak{F}})
of an FNN
{\mathfrak{F}}
is the depth of its
skeleton, that is, the length of a longest path
from an input node to an output node of
{\mathfrak{F}}
.

The
weight

\wt({\mathfrak{N}})
of a GNN
{\mathfrak{N}}
is the sum of the weights of the FNNs for the message and
combination functions of all layers of
{\mathfrak{N}}
.
The
bitsize

\bsize({\mathfrak{N}})
and the
size

\size({\mathfrak{N}})
of a rational piecewise linear GNN

{\mathfrak{N}}
is the sum of the (bit)sizes of all its FNNs. The

skeleton
of a GNN
{\mathfrak{N}}
consists of the directed acyclic graphs
underlying the FNNs for the message and combination functions of all
layers of
{\mathfrak{N}}
.
Thus if two GNNs have the same skeleton they have the same number of layers and the same input
and output dimensions on all layers, but they
may have different activation functions and different weights. The
depth

\depth({\mathfrak{N}})
of a
GNN
{\mathfrak{N}}
is the number of layers of
{\mathfrak{N}}
times the
maximum depth of all its FNNs.

Let

{\mathcal{N}}=({\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a family of GNNs. Suppose that the input dimension of
{\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)}
is
p^{(n)}
and the
output dimension is
q^{(n)}
. It will be convenient to call
(p^{(n)})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
the

input dimension
of
{\mathcal{N}}
and
(q^{(n)})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
the

output dimension
.
Then for every graph
G
of order
n
and every
\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p(n)}(G)

we let

{\mathcal{N}}(G,\mathcal{x})\coloneqq{\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)}(G,\mathcal{x})
and

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}(G,\mathcal{x})\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)}(G,\mathcal{x})
. Thus
{\mathcal{N}}
computes
a generalised form of signal transformation where the input and output
dimension depend on the order of the input graph.

We say that
{\mathcal{N}}
is of
polynomial weight
if there is a
polynomial
\pi(X)
such that
\wt({\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)})\leq\pi(n)
for all

n
.
Polynomial (bit)size
is defined similarly. The family
{\mathcal{N}}
is of

bounded depth
if there is a
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that

\depth({\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)})\leq d
for all
n
. The family

{\mathcal{N}}
is
rpl approximable
if there is a polynomial
\pi^{\prime}(X,Y)

such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
and all
\varepsilon>0
, every activation
function of
{\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)}
is
\varepsilon
-approximable by a rational
piecewise linear function of bitsize at most

\pi^{\prime}(\varepsilon^{-1},n)
.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be an rpl-approximable polynomial-weight, bounded-depth
family of GNNs of input dimension
(p^{(n)})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and
output dimension
(q^{(n)})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
. Let
\boldsymbol{X}
be an r-schema of type

{\texttt{v}\texttt{n}\to\texttt{r}}
, and let
W,W^{\prime}
be function variables of type

\emptyset\to\texttt{n}
.

Then there is
an r-expression
\textsf{gnn-eval}(x,y)
in
{\textsf{GFO+C}_{\textup{nu}}}
such that the following
holds for all graphs
G
and assignments
\mathcal{a}
over
G
. Let

n\coloneqq|G|
, and let

\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p^{(n)}}(G)
be the signal defined by

\mathcal{x}(v)\coloneqq\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,0),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,p^{(n)}-1)\Big).

(47)

Assume that

\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\mathcal{a}(W)
and that
\mathcal{a}(W^{\prime})\neq 0
. Let
\mathcal{y}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}(G,\mathcal{x})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{q^{(n)}}(G)
. Then for
all
v\in V(G)
,

\left\|\mathcal{y}(v)-\big(\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,0),\ldots,\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,q^{(n)}-1)\big)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{\mathcal{a}(W^{\prime})}.

(48)

Observe that Theorem
6.2
implies
Theorem
6.1
, because we can simply let
{\mathcal{N}}
be the
family consisting of the same GNN for every
n
. So we only need to
prove Theorem
6.2
.
The basic idea of the proof is simple. We exploit the continuity of the functions computed by FNNs
and GNNs not only in terms of the input signals but also in terms of
the weights and the biases. This allows us to approximate the
functions computed by GNNs with arbitrary real weights by GNNs with
rational weights. However, the bitsize of the rationals we need to get
a sufficiently precise approximation depends on the size of the input
graph, and this leads to the non-uniformity.

Before we delve into the proof, let us state one important corollary.
Extending the definition for single GNNs in the obvious way, we say
that a family
{\mathcal{N}}=({\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of GNNs
computes
a
unary query
{{\mathcal{Q}}}:{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}^{\textup{bool}}_{p}\to{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}^{\textup{bool}}_{1}
on
p
-labelled graphs if
for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, all
(G,\mathcal{b})\in{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}_{p}^{\textup{bool}}
with
n=|G|
, and
all
v\in V(G)
it holds that

\begin{cases}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)}(G,\mathcal{b})(v)\geq\frac{3}{4}&\text{if }{{\mathcal{Q}}}(G,\mathcal{b})(v)=1,\\
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)}(G,\mathcal{b})(v)\leq\frac{1}{4}&\text{if }{{\mathcal{Q}}}(G,\mathcal{b})(v)=0.\end{cases}

Corollary 6.3
.

Every unary query on
{\mathcal{G}\!\mathcal{S}}^{\textup{bool}}_{p}
that is computable by an
rpl-approximable polynomial-weight bounded-depth family of GNNs is
definable in
{\textsf{GFO+C}_{\textup{nu}}}
.

The exact analogues of Theorems
6.1
and

6.2
hold for GNNs with global readout and the
logic
\textsf{GFO+C}^{\textup{gc}}
, with only small modifications of the proof. For later
reference, we state the analogue of Theorem
6.2
.

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be an rpl-approximable polynomial-weight, bounded-depth
family of GNNs with global readout of input dimension
(p^{(n)})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and
output dimension
(q^{(n)})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
. Let
\boldsymbol{X}
be an r-schema of type

{\texttt{v}\texttt{n}\to\texttt{r}}
, and let
W,W^{\prime}
be function variables of type

\emptyset\to\texttt{n}
.

Then there is
an r-expression
\textsf{gnn-eval}(x,y)
in
{\textsf{GFO+C}^{\textup{gc}}_{\textup{nu}}}
such that the following
holds for all graphs
G
and assignments
\mathcal{a}
over
G
. Let

n\coloneqq|G|
, and let

\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p^{(n)}}(G)
be the signal defined by

\mathcal{x}(v)\coloneqq\Big(\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,0),\ldots,\left\llangle\boldsymbol{X}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,p^{(n)}-1)\Big).

(49)

Assume that

\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\mathcal{a}(W)
and that
\mathcal{a}(W^{\prime})\neq 0
. Let
\mathcal{y}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}(G,\mathcal{x})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{q^{(n)}}(G)
. Then for
all
v\in V(G)
,

\left\|\mathcal{y}(v)-\big(\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,0),\ldots,\left\llangle\textsf{gnn-eval}\right\rrangle^{(G,\mathcal{a})}(v,q^{(n)}-1)\big)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{\mathcal{a}(W^{\prime})}.

(50)

Remark 6.5
.

As the uniform simulation results, the nonuniform
Theorems
6.1
and
6.2
as well as Corollary
6.3
have
versions for 1-GNNs and the modal fragment
MFO+C
. In
Theorem
6.1
, if
{\mathfrak{N}}
is a 1-GNN then we obtain
r-expressions
\textsf{gnn-eval}_{i}(x)
in
\textsf{MFO+C}_{\textup{nu}}
, the modal fragment
with built-in numerical relations. Similarly, in
Theorem
6.2
, if
{\mathcal{N}}
is a family of 1-GNN then
we obtain an
r-expression
\textsf{gnn-eval}(x,y)
in
\textsf{MFO+C}_{\textup{nu}}
. And the modified
version of Corollary
6.3
states that queries
computable by
rpl-approximable polynomial-weight bounded-depth families of 1-GNNs
are definable in
\textsf{MFO+C}_{\textup{nu}}
.

As the modified versions of both Theorem
6.1
and
Corollary
6.3
follow easily from the modified
version of Theorem
6.2
, we only need to adapt the
proof of Theorem
6.2
. Within this long proof, the
only place where the exact messaging mechanism plays a role is in
the proof of Claim
6.27
(on page
6.27
). The changes we
need to make there are analogous to the changes we needed to make in
the proof of the modified version of Lemma
5.2
(see
Remark
5.8
).

6.1
Bounds and Approximations for FNNs

In this section, we shall prove that we can approximate rpl
approximable FNNs by rational piecewise linear FNNs whose size is
bounded in terms of the approximation ratio. For this, we first need
to establish bounds on the Lipschitz constant and growth of
an FNN in terms of its structure, its activation functions, and its
parameters.

Throughout this section, we let
{\mathfrak{A}}=\big(V,E,({\mathfrak{a}}_{v})_{v\in V}\big)

be an FNN architecture of input dimension
p
and output dimension
q
. We let
d
be the depth and
\Delta
the
maximum in-degree of the directed graph
(V,E)
. Without loss of
generality, we assume
\Delta\geq 1
and thus
d\geq 1
. If
\Delta=0
,
we simply add a dummy edge of weight
0
to the network.
Moreover, we let

\lambda\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
be a Lipschitz constant for all
activation function
{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}
for
v\in V
, and we let

\mu\coloneqq\max\big\{\left\lceil|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}(0)|\right\rceil\mathrel{\big|}v\in V\big\}.

For vectors
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
,
\boldsymbol{w}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{E}
,
\boldsymbol{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{V}
, we assume that
\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{p})
,

\boldsymbol{w}=(w_{e})_{e\in E}
, and
\boldsymbol{b}=(b_{v})_{v\in V}
.

In the first two lemmas we analyse the dependence of the growth and variation of
the functions
f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{b})
and

{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{b})
on the constants

d,\Delta,\lambda,\mu
and
\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}
(more precisely than in Lemma
2.6
).

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
\gamma\coloneqq 2\Delta\lambda\max\{\lambda,\mu\}
. Then for all
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
,
\boldsymbol{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{V}
,
and
\boldsymbol{w}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{E}
, and all
v\in V
of depth
t
we have

\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|\leq\gamma^{t}(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1).

(51)

Proof 6.7
.

Note that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
we have

|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}(x)|\leq\lambda|x|+\mu.

(52)

For all input nodes

X_{i}
we have

\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},X_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|=|x_{i}|\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}.

(53)

This implies (
51
) for
t=0
.

{techclaim}

For all nodes
v\in V
of depth
t\geq 1
we have

\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|\leq\big(\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{t}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\big(\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{s}\big(\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+\mu\big).

(54)

{subproof}

We prove
(
54
) by induction on
t\geq 1
.
Suppose that
v\in V
is a node of depth
t
, and let

v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}
be its in-neighbours. Let
b\coloneqq b_{v}
and

w_{i}\coloneqq w_{v_{i}v}
for
i\in[k]
. Moreover, let

y_{i}\coloneqq f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})
and

\boldsymbol{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})
. If
t=1
, by (
53
) we have

\left\|\boldsymbol{y}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}

(55)

If
t>1
, by the induction hypothesis we have

\left\|\boldsymbol{y}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\big(\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{t-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\sum_{s=0}^{t-2}\big(\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{s}\big(\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+\mu\big).

(56)

Thus

\displaystyle\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\lambda\Big|b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big|+\mu\Bigg|

by

(
52
)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{k}|w_{i}|\cdot|y_{i}|+\lambda|b|+\mu

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\Delta\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{y}\right\|_{\infty}+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+\mu.

Now if
t=1
, assertion (
54
) follows immediately from
(
55
). If
t>1
, by (
56
)
we obtain

\displaystyle\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{y}\right\|_{\infty}+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+\mu

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}\Big(\big(\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{t-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\sum_{s=0}^{t-2}\big(\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{s}\big(\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+\mu\big)\Big)+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+\mu

\displaystyle=\big(\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{t}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\big(\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{s}\big(\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+\mu\big).

This proves the claim.

It remains to prove that the claim yields (
51
) for
t\geq 1
. Since

\Delta\lambda\geq 1
, we have

\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\big(\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{s}\leq\big(2\Delta\lambda(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1))^{t}.

Thus by Claim
6.7
we have

\displaystyle\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big(2\Delta\lambda(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+\mu\big)

\displaystyle\leq\big(2\Delta\lambda(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t}\max\{\lambda,\mu\}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big).

Lemma 6.8
.

For all
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
,
\boldsymbol{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{V}
,
and
\boldsymbol{w}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{E}
it holds that

\left\|{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{b})-{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{b})\right\|_{\infty}\leq(\lambda\Delta)^{d}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{d}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}

Proof 6.9
.

Let
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
,
\boldsymbol{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{V}
,
and
\boldsymbol{w}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{E}
.
We shall prove by induction on
t
that for all nodes
v\in V
of
depth
t
we have

\left\|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{b})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{b})\right\|_{\infty}\leq(\lambda\Delta\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{t}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}.

(57)

Nodes of depth
t=0
are input nodes, and we have

\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},X_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}},X_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|=|x_{i}-x_{i}^{\prime}|\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}.

For the inductive step, let
v\in V
be a node of depth
t>0
, and
let
v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}
be its in-neighbours. Let
b\coloneqq b_{v},b^{\prime}\coloneqq b^{\prime}_{v}
and
w_{i}\coloneqq w_{v_{i}v},w^{\prime}_{i}\coloneqq w^{\prime}_{v_{i}v}
for
i\in[k]
. Moreover, let
\boldsymbol{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})
and
\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}=(y_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,y_{k}^{\prime})
with

y_{i}\coloneqq f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})
and

y_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})
. Then

\displaystyle\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)-{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}|y_{i}-y_{i}^{\prime}|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\Delta\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}.

Since by the induction hypothesis we have
\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq(\lambda\Delta\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty})^{t-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}
, the
assertion (
57
) follows.

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
\nu\coloneqq(4\Delta\lambda\gamma)^{d}
, where
\gamma
is the
constant of Lemma
6.6
. Then for
all
\varepsilon\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
,
\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{V}
,
and
\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{E}
with

0\leq\max\big\{\|\boldsymbol{b}-\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}\|_{\infty},\|\boldsymbol{w}-\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\big\}\leq\varepsilon\leq 1

(58)

we have

\big\|{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\nu\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{d}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

Proof 6.11
.

Let
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
and

\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{V}
,

\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{E}
such that (
58
) holds.

We shall prove by induction on
t
that for all nodes
v\in V
of
depth
t
we have

\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\big|\leq\big(4\Delta\lambda\gamma(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

(59)

Applied to the output nodes
v=Y_{i}
of depth
\leq d
, this yields the assertion of
the lemma.

Nodes of depth
t=0
are input nodes, and we have

\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},X_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}},X_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\big|=|x_{i}-x_{i}|=0.

(60)

For the inductive step, let
v\in V
be a node of depth
t>0
, and
let
v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}
be its in-neighbours. Let
b\coloneqq b_{v},b^{\prime}\coloneqq b^{\prime}_{v}
and
w_{i}\coloneqq w_{v_{i}v},w^{\prime}_{i}\coloneqq w^{\prime}_{v_{i}v}
for
i\in[k]
. Moreover, let
\boldsymbol{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})
and
\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}=(y_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,y_{k}^{\prime})
with

y_{i}\coloneqq f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})
and

y_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})
.

{techclaim}

\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\big|\leq\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+\Delta\lambda\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{y}\right\|_{\infty}+\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon

{subproof}

By the definition of
f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}
and the Lipschitz continuity of the
activation functions we have

\displaystyle\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)-{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}\Big(b^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\cdot\Big(|b-b^{\prime}|+\sum_{i=1}^{k}|w_{i}y_{i}-w_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime}|\Big)

Observe that
|b-b^{\prime}|\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{b}-\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon
and

\displaystyle y_{i}w_{i}-y_{i}^{\prime}w_{i}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(y_{i}-y_{i}^{\prime})w_{i}+y_{i}^{\prime}(w_{i}-w_{i}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=(y_{i}-y_{i}^{\prime})w_{i}+(y_{i}^{\prime}-y_{i})(w_{i}-w_{i}^{\prime})+y_{i}(w_{i}-w_{i}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon\|\boldsymbol{y}\|_{\infty}.

Hence

\displaystyle\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\Big(\varepsilon+\Delta\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\varepsilon+\|\boldsymbol{y}\|_{\infty}\varepsilon\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+\Delta\lambda\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{y}\right\|_{\infty}+\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

This proves the claim.

By the inductive hypothesis (
59
), we have

\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\big(4\Delta\lambda\gamma(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t-1}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

(61)

Thus

\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 4^{t-1}\big(\Delta\lambda\gamma(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon

(62)

and

\displaystyle\Delta\lambda\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\lambda\big(4\Delta\lambda\gamma(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t-1}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4^{t-1}\big(\Delta\lambda\gamma(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon

\displaystyle\text{because }\varepsilon\leq 1.

(63)

By Lemma
6.6
we have
\left\|\boldsymbol{y}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma^{t-1}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{t-1}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1)

and thus

\displaystyle\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{y}\right\|_{\infty}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\lambda\gamma^{t-1}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{t-1}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\big(\Delta\lambda\gamma(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon

(64)

Plugging (
62
), (
63
), and (
64
) into
Claim
6.11
, we get

\displaystyle\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\big|\leq\;

\displaystyle 4^{t-1}\big(\Delta\lambda\gamma(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon

\displaystyle+(\Delta\lambda\gamma(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1))^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\varepsilon

\displaystyle+4^{t-1}\big(\Delta\lambda\gamma(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon

\displaystyle+\Delta\lambda\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle 4^{t}\big(\Delta\lambda\gamma(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

This proves (
59
) and thus completes the proof of the lemma.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
\beta\coloneqq(4\Delta\lambda\gamma)^{d}
, where
\gamma
is the
constant of Lemma
6.6
. Let
\varepsilon>0
. For all
v\in V
, let
{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}^{\prime}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be an

\varepsilon
-approximation of

{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}
that is Lipschitz continuous with constant
2\lambda
, and
let
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}\coloneqq(V,E,({\mathfrak{a}}_{v}^{\prime})_{v\in V})
. Then for
all
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
,
\boldsymbol{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{V}
,
and
\boldsymbol{w}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{E}
,

\big\|{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\beta\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{d}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

Proof 6.13
.

We shall prove by induction on
t
that for all nodes
v\in V
of
depth
t
we have

\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|\leq(4\Delta\lambda\gamma)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

(65)

This yields the assertion of
the lemma.

Nodes of depth
t=0
are input nodes, and we have

\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},X_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime},X_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|=|x_{i}-x_{i}|=0.

For the inductive step, let
v\in V
be a node of depth
t>0
, and
let
v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}
be its in-neighbours. Let
b\coloneqq b_{v}
and
w_{i}\coloneqq w_{v_{i}v}
for
i\in[k]
. Moreover, let
\boldsymbol{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})
and
\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}=(y_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,y_{k}^{\prime})
with

y_{i}\coloneqq f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})
and

y_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq f_{{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime},v_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})
.

{techclaim}

\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|\leq 2\gamma^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|+1\big)^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\varepsilon+2\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}

{subproof}

We have

\displaystyle\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)-{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)-{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)\Big|

(66)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\Big|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}^{\prime}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)-{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\Big)\Big|

By Lemma
6.6
we have

\Big|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)\Big|=\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|\leq\gamma^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|+1\big)^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1).

(67)

Since
{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v}

\varepsilon
-approximates
{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}
, this implies

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)-{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\gamma^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|+1\big)^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1)+\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|+1\big)^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\varepsilon.

(68)

Furthermore, by the Lipschitz continuity of
{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v}
we have

\Big|{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}^{\prime}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y_{i}\Big)-{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v}\Big(b+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\Big)\Big|\leq 2\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{k}|w_{i}|\cdot|y_{i}-y_{i}^{\prime}|\leq 2\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}.

(69)

The assertion of the claim from (
66
), (
68
), and (
69
).

By the inductive hypothesis (
65
), we have

\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq(4\Delta\lambda\gamma)^{t-1}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{t-1}\big(\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

Thus by the claim,

\displaystyle\big|f_{{\mathfrak{A}},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-f_{{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime},v}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|+1\big)^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1)\varepsilon+2\Delta\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{y}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|+1\big)^{t}\big(\|\boldsymbol{x}\|_{\infty}+\|\boldsymbol{b}\|_{\infty}+1)\varepsilon

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+2\cdot 4^{t-1}(\Delta\lambda\gamma)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq(4\gamma\Delta\lambda)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{t}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

This proves (
65
) and hence the lemma.

Lemma 6.14
.

Let
f:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be Lipschitz continuous with constant

\lambda>0
such that
f
is rpl approximable. Then for every

\varepsilon>0
there is a rational piecewise linear function
L
of
bitsize polynomial in
\varepsilon^{-1}
such that
L
is an

\varepsilon
-approximation of
f
and
L
is Lipschitz continuous with
constant
(1+\varepsilon)\lambda
.

Proof 6.15
.

Let
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}\coloneqq\frac{\varepsilon}{10}
. Let
L^{\prime}
be a piecewise linear

\varepsilon^{\prime}
-approximation of
f
of bitsize polynomial in

\varepsilon^{-1}
. Let
t_{1}<\ldots<t_{n}
be the thresholds of
L^{\prime}
, and
let
a_{0},\ldots,a_{n}
and
b_{0},\ldots,b_{n}
be its slopes
and constants. Then
|a_{0}|\leq(1+\varepsilon)\lambda
; otherwise the
slope of the linear function
a_{0}x+b_{0}
would be too large (in
absolute value) to approximate the function
f
whose slope is
bounded by
\lambda
. For the same reason,

|a_{n}^{\prime}|\leq(1+\varepsilon)\lambda
.

Let
s\coloneqq t_{1}
and
s^{\prime}\coloneqq t_{n}
. We subdivide the
interval
[s,s^{\prime}]
into sufficiently small subintervals (of length at
most
\varepsilon^{\prime}\cdot\lambda^{-1}
). Within each such interval,
f

does not change much, because it is Lipschitz continuous, and we can
approximate it sufficiently closely by a linear function with
parameters whose bitsize is polynomially bounded in

\varepsilon^{-1}
. The slope of theses linear functions will not be
significantly larger than
\lambda
, because the slope of
f
is
bounded by
\lambda
. We can combine all these linear pieces with
the linear functions
a_{0}x+b_{0}
for the interval
(-\infty,s]
and

a_{n}x+b_{n}
for the interval
[s^{\prime},\infty)
to obtain the desired
piecewise linear approximation of
f
.

Now we are ready to prove the main result of this subsection.

Lemma 6.16
.

For every
d\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
there is a polynomial
\pi^{\prime}(X,Y)
such that the
following holds.
Let
{\mathfrak{F}}=\big(V,E,({\mathfrak{a}}_{v})_{v\in V},\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{b}\big)
be an
rpl-approximable FNN
architecture of depth
d
. Let

\varepsilon>0
. Then there exists a rational piecewise-linear FNN

{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}=\big(V,E,({\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v})_{v\in V},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}\big)
of size at most
\pi^{\prime}\big(\varepsilon^{-1},\wt({\mathfrak{F}})\big)

such that for all
v\in V
it holds that
\lambda({\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v})\leq 2\lambda({\mathfrak{a}}_{v})
and for all
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
it holds that

\left\|{\mathfrak{F}}(\boldsymbol{x})-{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

Note that
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}
has the same skeleton as
{\mathfrak{F}}
.

Proof 6.17
.

Without loss of generality we assume
\varepsilon\leq 1
.
Let
{\mathfrak{A}}\coloneqq\big(V,E,({\mathfrak{a}}_{v})_{v\in V}\big)
. Define the parameters
\Delta,\lambda,\mu
with
respect to
{\mathfrak{A}}
as before. Note that

\Delta,\lambda,\mu\leq\wt({\mathfrak{F}})
.
Choose the constants
\gamma
according to Lemma
6.6
,
\nu
according to
Lemma
6.10
, and
\beta
according to
Lemma
6.12
and note that for fixed
d
they depend
polynomially on
\Delta,\lambda,\mu
and hence on
\wt({\mathfrak{F}})
.

Let

\alpha\coloneqq 2\nu\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{d}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)
. Let
\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\big[{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\big]^{E}
,
\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\big[{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\big]^{V}
such
that
\|\boldsymbol{w}-\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\alpha}
and

\|\boldsymbol{b}-\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\alpha}
. Clearly, we
can choose such
\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}=(w_{e}^{\prime})_{e\in E}
and

\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}=(b^{\prime}_{v})_{v\in V}
such that all their entries have bitsize
polynomial in
\frac{\alpha}{\varepsilon}
, which is polynomial in

\varepsilon^{-1}
and in
\wt({\mathfrak{F}})
. Then by Lemma
6.10
,
for all
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
we have

\left\|{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\nu\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{d}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\frac{\varepsilon}{\alpha}\leq\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\frac{\varepsilon}{2},

Let
\alpha^{\prime}\coloneqq 2\beta\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{d}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)
.
For every
v\in V
, we let
{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v}
be a rational piecwise-linear
function of bitsize polynomial
in in
\varepsilon^{-1}
that is an

\frac{\varepsilon}{\alpha^{\prime}}
-approximation of
{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}
and Lipschitz
continuous with constant
2\lambda
. Such an
{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v}
exists by
Lemma
6.14
, because
{\mathfrak{a}}_{v}

is rpl-approximable and Lipschitz continuous with constant
\lambda
. Let
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}\coloneqq\big(V,E,({\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}_{v})_{v\in V}\big)
. By Lemma
6.12
, for all
\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
we have

\left\|{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})-{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\beta\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)^{d}\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\frac{\varepsilon}{\alpha^{\prime}}\leq\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

Overall,

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathfrak{F}}(\boldsymbol{x})-{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{w})-{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|{\mathfrak{A}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})-{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

6.2
Bounds and Approximations for GNNs

We start with a more explicit version of Lemmas
4.2
and
4.4
,
the growth bounds for GNN layers. Recall that the depth of a GNN layer
is the maximum of the depths of the FNNs for the combination and the
message function.

Lemma 6.18
.

For every
d\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
there is a polynomial
\pi(X)
such that the following
holds. Let
{\mathfrak{L}}
be a GNN layer of depth
d
, and let
p

be the input dimension of
{\mathfrak{L}}
. Then for all
graphs
G
and all signals
\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\pi\big(\wt({\mathfrak{L}})\big)\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)|G|.

(70)

Proof 6.19
.

The proof of Lemma
4.2
yields

\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})(v)\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2\gamma_{\textsf{msg}}\gamma_{\textsf{comb}}\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)|G|,

where
\gamma_{\textsf{msg}}
,

\gamma_{\textsf{comb}}
are growth bounds for the message and
combination functions of
{\mathfrak{L}}
. It follows from Lemma
6.6

that
\gamma_{\textsf{msg}}
,

\gamma_{\textsf{comb}}
can be chosen polynomial in the weight of
{\mathfrak{L}}
.

Lemma 6.20
.

For every
d\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
there is a polynomial
\pi(X)
such that the following
holds. Let
{\mathfrak{L}}
be a GNN layer of depth
d
, and let
p

be the input dimension of
{\mathfrak{L}}
. Then for all
graphs
G
and all signals
\mathcal{x},\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})-\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\pi\big(\wt({\mathfrak{L}})\big)\left\|\mathcal{x}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}|G|.

(71)

Proof 6.21
.

The proof of Lemma
4.4
yields

\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})(v)-\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x}^{\prime})(v)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{\textsf{msg}}\lambda_{\textsf{comb}}\left\|\mathcal{x}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}|G|,

where
\lambda_{\textsf{msg}}
,

\lambda_{\textsf{comb}}
are Lipschitz constants for the message and
combination functions of
{\mathfrak{L}}
. It follows from Lemma
6.8

that
\lambda_{\textsf{msg}}
,

\lambda_{\textsf{comb}}
can be chosen polynomial in the weight of
{\mathfrak{L}}
.

Corollary 6.22
.

For every
d\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
there is a polynomial
\pi(X)
such that the following
holds. Let
{\mathfrak{N}}
be a GNN of depth
d
, and let
p

be the input dimension of
{\mathfrak{N}}
. Then for all
graphs
G
and all signals
\mathcal{x},\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}(G,\mathcal{x})-\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}(G,\mathcal{x}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\pi\big(\wt({\mathfrak{L}})\big)\left\|\mathcal{x}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}|G|^{d}.

Lemma 6.23
.

For every
d\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
there exist polynomials
\pi(X)
and

\pi^{\prime}(X,Y)
such that the following holds. Let
{\mathfrak{L}}
be an
rpl-approximable GNN layer of depth
d
, and let
p
be the input
dimension of
{\mathfrak{L}}
. Then for all
\varepsilon>0
there exists a
rational piecewise-linear GNN layer
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
of size at most

\pi^{\prime}(\varepsilon^{-1},\wt({\mathfrak{L}}))
with the same skeleton as
{\mathfrak{L}}
such
that the Lipschitz constants of the activation functions of
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}

are at most twice the Lipschitz constants of the corresponding
activation functions in
{\mathfrak{L}}
and for all graphs
G
, all signals

\mathcal{x},\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
, and all vertices
v\in V(G)
it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})-\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}(G,\mathcal{x}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\pi\big(\wt({\mathfrak{L}})\big)|G|\Big(\left\|\mathcal{x}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon\Big).

Proof 6.24
.

Let
\textsf{msg}:{\mathbb{R}}^{2p}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r}
and
\textsf{comb}:{\mathbb{R}}^{p+r}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{q}
be
the message and combination functions of
{\mathfrak{L}}
, and let
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{msg}}

and
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{comb}}
be the FNNs for these functions. By
Lemma
6.16
there are rational piecewise linear FNNs

{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}_{\textsf{msg}}
and
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}_{\textsf{comb}}
of size polynomial in
\wt({\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{msg}}),\wt({\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{comb}})\leq\wt({\mathfrak{L}})

with activation functions of Lipschitz constants at most twice the Lipschitz constants of the
corresponding activation functions in
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{msg}}
,
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{comb}}
such that for all

\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
and
\boldsymbol{z}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{r}
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\textsf{msg}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})-\textsf{msg}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\left\|(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon,

(72)

\displaystyle\left\|\textsf{comb}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{z})-\textsf{comb}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{z})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\left\|(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{z})\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

(73)

Let
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
be the GNN layer with message function
\textsf{msg}^{\prime}
,
combination function
\textsf{comb}^{\prime}
, and the same aggregation function

agg
as
{\mathfrak{L}}
. By Lemma
6.6
there is an
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}

that is polynomial in
\wt({\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{msg}})
and hence polynomial in

\wt({\mathfrak{L}})
such that
for all
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
it holds that

\left\|\textsf{msg}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\alpha\big(\max\big\{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\big\}+1\big).

(74)

By Lemma
6.8
there is an
\alpha^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
polynomial in
\wt({\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{comb}}^{\prime})
and hence polynomial in

\wt({\mathfrak{L}})
such that
for all
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p},\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{r}
it holds that

\left\|\textsf{comb}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{z})-\textsf{comb}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\alpha^{\prime}\max\big\{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\boldsymbol{z}-\boldsymbol{z}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\big\}.

(75)

By Lemma
6.20
there is an
\alpha^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
polynomial in

\wt({\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime})
and thus polynomial in
\wt({\mathfrak{L}})
such
that for all
G
and
\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
,

\left\|\widetilde{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\prime}(G,\boldsymbol{x})-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\prime}(G,\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}\left\|\mathcal{x}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}|G|.

(76)

Let
G
be a graph of order
n\coloneqq|G|
and
v\in V(G)
. Furthermore, let
\mathcal{x},\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
and
\mathcal{y}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})
,

\mathcal{y}^{\prime}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}(G,\mathcal{x})
,

\mathcal{y}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}(G,\mathcal{x}^{\prime})
. Then

\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(G,\mathcal{x})(v)-\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}(G,\mathcal{x}^{\prime})(v)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\left\|\mathcal{y}(v)-\mathcal{y}^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\mathcal{y}^{\prime}(v)-\mathcal{y}^{\prime\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}.

By (
76
) we have

\left\|\mathcal{y}^{\prime}(v)-\mathcal{y}^{\prime\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}n\left\|\mathcal{x}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}.

(77)

Thus we need to
bound
\left\|\mathcal{y}(v)-\mathcal{y}^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}
. Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{z}(v)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\textsf{agg}\Big({\big\{\hskip-4.3pt\big\{}\textsf{msg}(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{x}(w))\mathrel{\big|}w\in N_{G}(v){\big\}\hskip-4.3pt\big\}}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{z}^{\prime}(v)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\textsf{agg}\Big({\big\{\hskip-4.3pt\big\{}\textsf{msg}^{\prime}(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{x}(w))\mathrel{\big|}w\in N_{G}(v){\big\}\hskip-4.3pt\big\}}\Big).

Then by (
72
), we have

\left\|\mathcal{z}(v)-\mathcal{z}^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}\leq n\left(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\right)\varepsilon.

(78)

Furthermore, by (
74
), for all
w\in N(v)
we have

\left\|\textsf{msg}\big(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{x}(w)\big)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\alpha\Big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\Big)

Thus, since
\alpha\geq 1
and
n\geq 1
,

\left\|\big(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}(v)\big)\right\|_{\infty}=\max\Big\{\left\|\mathcal{x}(v)\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\mathcal{z}(v)\right\|_{\infty}\Big\}\leq\alpha n\Big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\Big).

(79)

Putting things together, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{y}(v)-\mathcal{y}^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\textsf{comb}\big(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}(v)\big)-\textsf{comb}^{\prime}\big(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}^{\prime}(v)\big)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\textsf{comb}\big(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}(v)\big)-\textsf{comb}^{\prime}\big(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}(v)\big)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\left\|\textsf{comb}^{\prime}\big(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}(v)\big)-\textsf{comb}^{\prime}\big(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}^{\prime}(v)\big)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\left\|(\mathcal{x}(v),\mathcal{z}(v))\right\|_{\infty}+1)\varepsilon

by
(
73
)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+\alpha^{\prime}\left\|\mathcal{z}(v)-\mathcal{z}^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{\infty}

by (
75
)

\displaystyle\leq 2\alpha n\Big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\Big)\varepsilon+\alpha^{\prime}n\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon

by
(
79
) and
(
78
)

\displaystyle\leq(2\alpha+\alpha^{\prime})n\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon

Combined with (
77
), this yields the assertion of the lemma.

Now we are ready to prove the main lemma of this section.

Lemma 6.25
.

For every
d\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
there exist a polynomial
\pi(X,Y)
such that
the following holds. Let
{\mathfrak{N}}
be an rpl-approximable GNN of depth

d
, and let
p
be the input dimension of
{\mathfrak{N}}
. Then for all

\varepsilon>0
there exists a rational piecewise-linear GNN
{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
of
size at most
\pi(\varepsilon^{-1},\wt({\mathfrak{N}}))
with the same skeleton
as
{\mathfrak{N}}
such that the Lipschitz constants of the activation
functions of
{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
are at most twice the Lipschitz constants of the
corresponding activation functions in
{\mathfrak{N}}
and for all graphs
G

and all signals
\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
it holds that

\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}(G,\mathcal{x})-\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}(G,\mathcal{x})\right\|_{\infty}\leq|G|^{d}\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon.

Proof 6.26
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{N}}=({\mathfrak{L}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathfrak{L}}_{d})
. For every
t\in[d]
, let

p_{t-1}
be the input dimension of
{\mathfrak{L}}_{t}
. Then
p=p_{0}
.
By Lemma
6.18
there is an

\alpha
polynomial in
\wt({\mathfrak{N}})
such that for all
t\in[d]
,
G
, and

\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p_{t-1}}(G)
we have

\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{t}(G,\mathcal{x})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\alpha|G|\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big).

(80)

Let
\pi^{\prime}(X)
be the polynomial of Lemma
6.23
,

\alpha^{\prime}\coloneqq\max_{t\in[d]}\pi^{\prime}\big(\wt({\mathfrak{L}}_{t})\big),

and

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle\coloneqq 3\max\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\},

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\varepsilon}{\beta^{d}}.

Note that
\varepsilon^{\prime}
is polynomial in
\wt({\mathfrak{N}})
. For every
t\in[d]
, we apply
Lemma
6.23
to
{\mathfrak{L}}_{t}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}
and obtain a
rational piecewise linear GNN layer

{\mathfrak{L}}_{t}^{\prime}
such that for all graphs
G
and all
signals
\mathcal{x},\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p_{t-1}}(G)
we have

\left\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{t}(G,\mathcal{x})-\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{t}^{\prime}(G,\mathcal{x}^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\alpha^{\prime}|G|\Big(\left\|\mathcal{x}-\mathcal{x}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon^{\prime}\Big).

(81)

Let
G
be a graph of order
n\coloneqq|G|
, and

\mathcal{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}(G)
.
Let
\mathcal{x}_{0}\coloneqq\mathcal{x}_{0}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{x}
, and for
t\in[d]
, let

\mathcal{x}_{t}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{t}(G,\mathcal{x}_{t-1})
and

\mathcal{x}_{t}^{\prime}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}_{i}(G,\mathcal{x}_{t-1}^{\prime})
. We shall prove that
for all
t\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{x}_{t}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\beta^{t}n^{t}\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big),

(82)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{x}_{t}-\mathcal{x}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\beta^{t}n^{t}\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon^{\prime}.

(83)

Since

\beta^{d}\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon
and
\mathcal{x}_{d}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}(G,\mathcal{x})
,

\mathcal{x}_{d}^{\prime}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}(G,\mathcal{x})
, (
83
) implies the
assertion of the lemma.

We prove (
82
) and (
83
) by induction on
t
. The
base step
t=0
is trivial, because
\mathcal{x}_{0}=\mathcal{x}
and

\mathcal{x}_{0}=\mathcal{x}^{\prime}
. For the inductive step, let
t\geq 1
.
By (
80
) and the induction hypothesis we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{x}_{t}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha n\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}_{t-1}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha n\Big(\beta^{t-1}n^{t-1}\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)+1\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\beta^{t}n^{t}\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big),

where the last inequality holds because
2\alpha\leq\beta
.
This proves (
82
).

By (
81
) we have

\left\|\mathcal{x}_{t}-\mathcal{x}_{t}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\alpha^{\prime}n\Big(\left\|\mathcal{x}_{t-1}-\mathcal{x}_{t-1}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}_{t-1}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon^{\prime}\Big).

(84)

By induction hypothesis (
83
),

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}n\left\|\mathcal{x}_{t-1}-\mathcal{x}_{t-1}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{\prime}n\beta^{t-1}n^{t-1}\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3}\beta^{t}n^{t}\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon^{\prime}.

(85)

By induction hypothesis (
82
),

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}n\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}_{t-1}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{\prime}n\Big(\beta^{t-1}n^{t-1}\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)+1\Big)\varepsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3}\beta^{t}n^{t}\big(\left\|\mathcal{x}\right\|_{\infty}+1\big)\varepsilon^{\prime}

(86)

Plugging (
85
) and (
86
) into (
84
), we obtain
the desired inequality (
83
).

6.3
Proof of Theorem
6.2

Let us first remark that we cannot directly apply
Theorem
5.1
(the “uniform theorem”) to a family of rational
piecewise linear GNN approximating the GNNs in our family
{\mathcal{N}}
. The reason is that in Theorem
5.1
the GNN is
“hardwired” in the formula, whereas in our non-uniform setting we
obtain a different GNN for every input size. Instead, we encode the
sequence of rational piecewise linear GNNs approximating the GNNs in
the original family into the numerical
built-in relations. Then our formula evaluates these GNNs directly on
the numerical side of the structures.

Proof 6.27

(Proof of Theorem
6.2
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}=({\mathfrak{N}}^{(n)})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
. Furthermore, let
d
be an upper
